````json
 },
 "limits": {
  "flash": {
   "note": "Photosensitive-seizure bounds. The face fills well over a quarter of the visual field at phone reading distance, so the small-area exemption in the guidance does not apply and these are hard limits, not advice. Enforced in three places: the randomiser will not generate parameters that break them, a governor inside resolve() holds the colour when a fourth opposing transition would land inside one second, and a build check replays every pattern at both parameter extremes. resolve() is the only path to a colour, so a hand-written binding cannot get past it.",
   "max_transitions_per_s": 3,
   "min_luma_delta": 0.1,
   "strobe_max_s": 2.0,
   "flicker_rate_hz_max": 1.3,
   "flicker_harmonic": 2.3,
   "why_these_numbers": "Three opposing transitions per second is the standard threshold. 0.10 relative luminance is the swing below which a transition is not counted. flicker's implementation adds a harmonic at 2.3x its stated rate, so the stated rate must stay below the limit divided by 2.3.",
   "scope": "per surface",
   "scope_why": "The governor keeps one window per SURFACE (and one for the UI swatches). A single page-wide window, fed by every surface in turn, saw twenty faces with twenty clocks as one face flashing between twenty colours, spent its budget on those cross-surface transitions and then held one face's colour on another. Found 14 Sep by the specimen rig; on the live grid it was the same fault with less contrast. A client that draws several reactors (a picker) must give each its own governor; a client that draws one needs one.",
   "enforced_in": {
    "randomiser": "will not generate parameters that break these limits (rate_hz clamped to flicker_rate_hz_max at generation)",
    "resolve": "_resolveRaw() clamps rate_hz to flicker_rate_hz_max and strobe period_s to 2/max_transitions_per_s AT THE POINT OF USE - so the limit holds for a hand-written binding, a future randomiser, or a spec that is missing the key",
    "governor": "lumaGovernor() holds the last on-screen colour when a fourth opposing transition would land inside one second, per surface",
    "build_check": "replays every pattern at both parameter extremes"
   },
   "found_14_sep": "The Android port checked shell.html against this file and found the third place was not enforcing. _resolveRaw() carried a SECOND copy of every pattern default as inline literals (`q.rate_hz || 7`), eleven of twenty had drifted from the spec, and `||` treats an explicit 0 as absent - so a binding carrying `rate_hz: 0` resolved to 7Hz, and 7 x the 2.3 harmonic is 16.1Hz, inside the photosensitive band this limit exists to exclude. Unreachable in normal use (the spec's params merge first) and the governor caught it downstream, so it was never an exposure - but two of the three places agreed and the third did not. Fixed by deleting the duplicated defaults entirely, reading every number through pnum() with `??`, and clamping the two safety-bearing parameters at the point of use. Measured after: rate_hz 0 -> 0 opposing transitions/s, rate_hz 40 -> 1/s, strobe period_s 0 -> 2.67/s, all under the limit of 3. The first version of the strobe clamp floored the period at 1/max_transitions_per_s and still ran at 5.67/s, because a strobe makes TWO transitions per period; the floor is 2/max_transitions_per_s."
  }
 },
 "state_transforms": {
  "note": "A face has FOUR motion tables and the product has EIGHT states, so four of them borrow. Borrowing alone was not enough, and for two states it was worse than nothing. These transforms are applied by the SHELL on top of a borrowed table - a clock multiplier, a direction, a dim, and at most one overlay - so all twenty faces behave identically and each client implements this once instead of twenty times.",
  "rate_ease_s": 0.22,
  "why_ease": "The spin RATE is eased, not snapped. A face whose rate steps from 0.22 to 1.35 in one frame reads as a cut even when the angle is continuous.",
  "phase": {
   "rule": "angle += dt * speed(state)",
   "why": "Never angle = clock * speed. Multiplying the absolute clock by a per-state speed makes the angle jump by clock * (new - old) the instant the state changes. Measured on this build before the fix: 434, 590, 773 and 255 radians on the four common transitions after fifteen minutes of uptime - between 40 and 123 full revolutions, on a single frame, at exactly the moment the owner is looking at the face. Integrating the rate is also what makes the eased ramp above continuous for free.",
   "applies_to": "spin only; a camera drift or a slow pitch oscillation stays on the raw clock"
  },
  "listen_floor": {
   "value": 0.28,
   "why": "What `listening` is worth with a silent microphone. Silence is not absence of attention: a person pauses between words, and a face that drops below idle during the pause reads as 'it stopped listening' at exactly the moment they check. The microphone level adds to this floor rather than replacing it."
  },
  "amp_envelope": {
   "attack_s": 0.03,
   "release_s": 0.22,
   "gate": 0.04,
   "why": "Raw microphone level is unusable: noisy at the top, and it falls off a cliff between syllables. Rise fast so the face confirms it heard you, fall slow so it holds through the gaps, gate underneath so room hum animates nothing. The constants are in SECONDS and converted per frame, so a 120Hz screen smooths by the same amount as a 60Hz one - a fixed per-frame lerp does not, and the feel of the thing changed with the refresh rate."
  },
  "states": {
   "approval": {
    "borrow": "idle",
    "rate": 1.0,
    "dir": 1,
    "dim": 1.0,
    "overlay": "ping",
    "overlay_spec": {
     "period_s": 1.6,
     "expand_s": 0.4,
     "ease": "out-cubic",
     "from_r": 0.12,
     "to_r": 0.94,
     "peak_alpha": 0.55
    },
    "why": "It used to borrow LISTENING, and a dozen faces do not merely read listening's parameters - they branch on the state name and multiply by the microphone level. During an approval the mic is not live, so those faces drew LESS than idle: the one state that has to pull the eye across a room was the deadest thing on the screen. It now borrows idle's pace and gets a ring that leaves the core every 1.6 seconds. That is a knock at a door - it repeats, it is directional, and it stops the moment you answer."
   },
   "error": {
    "borrow": "thinking",
    "rate": 0.3,
    "dir": -1,
    "dim": 0.9,
    "overlay": "hitch",
    "envelope_s": {
     "hitch": 0.06,
     "hold": 0.25,
     "shake": 0.3
    },
    "shake": {
     "amplitude_frac": 0.015,
     "hz": 8,
     "kind": "positional",
     "why_positional": "a brightness shake at 8Hz is a flash; moving the face a pixel or two is just as legible and carries no photosensitivity risk"
    },
    "why": "It used to borrow THINKING, so an error looked like harder thinking - the same fast agitated motion, distinguished only by colour. Colour alone fails for the one man in twelve with a red-green deficiency, at a glance from across a room, and in any frozen frame or thumbnail. Error now HITCHES: the spin stops dead in 60ms, holds, shakes, then crawls BACKWARDS at 0.3x idle. Nothing else in this product ever runs backwards, so the direction is the signal - and direction survives colour blindness, a still frame and peripheral vision."
   },
   "standby": {
    "borrow": "idle",
    "rate": 0.5,
    "dir": 1,
    "dim": 0.6,
    "overlay": null,
    "why": "Idle at half pace, dimmed. This one was always right."
   },
   "banked": {
    "borrow": "idle",
    "rate": 0.0,
    "dir": 1,
    "dim": 0.45,
    "overlay": "notches",
    "overlay_spec": {
     "ring_r": 0.93,
     "notch_from_r": 0.86,
     "notch_to_r": 0.99,
     "start": "12 o'clock",
     "direction": "clockwise",
     "max_notches": 12,
     "overflow_mark": true,
     "source": "the number of items waiting in the digest (jarvis_arbiter.pending_count)",
     "radius_basis": "0.44 * min(w,h) - the rim of the FACE, not of the buffer",
     "note": "Radii are fractions of the radius basis above. A ring at the full half-width sits outside the artwork and clips against the canvas edge once the stroke width is added."
    },
    "why": "Still - not slow, STOPPED. It means 'there are things waiting and I am not going to say them out loud', and the number of things is the one piece of information the state exists to carry, so it is drawn as notches around the rim. On Android the frame clock should actually stop rather than redrawing an unchanging frame 120 times a second; a change in the notch count wakes it for exactly one frame."
   }
  },
  "dim_rule": {
   "how": "blend toward the face's own background colour, never multiply toward zero",
   "why": "A multiply lands banked's already-dark colour around 0.002 relative luminance, which many OLED panels quantise to black - so the state that means 'something is waiting' would vanish on the device most likely to show it."
  },
  "color_ease_s": 0.3,
  "color_ease_why": "Every state change used to cut in colour on the frame of the switch (the destination hue at +50ms in every row of every transition strip). The shell now fades from the colour actually being shown to the new state's colour over 300ms with a smoothstep. A face that changes hue in one frame reads as a different object; one that fades reads as the same object changing its mind.",
  "tint_delay_s": {
   "approval": 0.1,
   "why": "The ring is seen to arrive before the colour follows - the knock comes before the door opens."
  },
  "approval_clock_s": 20,
  "approval_overlay": "clock: a rim arc from twelve o'clock closing clockwise over approval_clock_s then held, drawn over the knock ping; the first 220ms sweep to 12% so the ring is on screen before the tint. Approval is thereby legible with no colour at all.",
  "speech_modulation": "see `speech`"
 },
 "color_slots": {
  "note": "Which of a face's colour slots receives the bright bound colour. This is matched BY NAME, not by declaration order. Order was the original rule and it was wrong for half the set: ten faces declare the structural colour first (col/glow), so the dim slot received the bright value and the hot accent received the darkened one. Every one of those faces was drawing its bound colour inside out.",
  "hot": [
   "hot",
   "a"
  ],
  "dim": [
   "col",
   "glow",
   "b"
  ],
  "other": "anything else (e.g. `neg`, a second contrasting hue) takes the midpoint"
 },
 "speech": {
  "note": "Jarvis's own voice, as distinct from the owner's microphone. The audit's largest finding: nothing talked - the speaking gallery was the idle gallery tinted. The client feeds the 0..1 level of the audio it is playing through setSpeechLevel(); the shell smooths it with a speech-shaped envelope and applies it to EVERY face as a scale push and a brightness lift, so all twenty talk without any face knowing how.",
  "api": "setSpeechLevel(level_0_to_1) - call per audio frame while TTS plays. On Android, feed it from the AudioTrack/Visualizer RMS; on the desktop from the Web Audio AnalyserNode of the playback element.",
  "envelope": {
   "attack_s": 0.04,
   "release_s": 0.12,
   "gate": 0.04,
   "why": "A syllable rises in ~40ms and decays in ~120ms; the gate keeps player hiss from animating anything."
  },
  "scale": 0.035,
  "scale_why": "Up to 3.5% growth on a loud syllable. Positional, not luminance, so it is not a flash.",
  "brightness_lift": 0.18,
  "speak_floor": 0.18,
  "speak_floor_why": "A speaking face in the gap between words still glows above idle.",
  "listening_uses_same_path": "The microphone envelope (attack 30ms / release 220ms) drives the same scale push while listening, so the owner's own voice visibly moves the face.",
  "fallback": "If no level has arrived for 0.5s while speaking, a synthetic envelope stands in: syllables at 4.2Hz with varying weight and a 220ms rest every 2.3s. Speaking is never frozen, even before TTS is wired."
 },
 "interaction": {
  "tap": {
   "ring_s": 0.15,
   "flinch_s": 0.15,
   "flinch_px_frac": 0.01,
   "ring_frac": 0.28,
   "why": "Within one frame of a touch, before the model has said anything, the face flinches ~1% of its width away from the finger and a thin ring in the state colour leaves the touch point and fades over 150ms. It is the 'I felt that' of a physical object, identical on every face and in every state."
  },
  "drag": {
   "yaw_per_px": 0.008,
   "pitch_per_px": 0.006,
   "pitch_limit_rad": 1.4,
   "inertia_tau_s": 0.45,
   "max_rad_s": 4,
   "why": "A released face keeps turning and settles instead of stopping dead under the finger."
  },
  "hover": "Iris looks toward the cursor (catchlight slides with TOUCH.x/y); other faces read TOUCH for their own hover cues.",
  "wheel_pinch": "zoom 0.45-3x; double-tap resets the view",
  "planned": [
   "hold = push-to-talk",
   "gyro parallax 2-3px on the rim only (phone)",
   "pinch to minimise to the 44px ring",
   "transcript orbiting the rim while speaking",
   "banked items as particles caught in the outer ring"
  ]
 },
 "framing": {
  "rule": "The active silhouette fits inside 70% of the shorter edge, leaving room for the HUD, the rim ring and the notches.",
  "fit_field": "faces[].render.fit - a uniform scale the shell applies around the draw. 1.0 for faces that already sit inside a circle with air around them.",
  "fit_values": {
   "shoal": 0.8,
   "cascade": 0.82,
   "iris": 0.86,
   "spectrum": 0.86,
   "kirkwood": 0.9,
   "nucleus": 0.92
  }
 },
 "picker": {
  "more_toggle": true,
  "archive_recommended": [
   "geodesic",
   "kirkwood"
  ],
  "archive_note": "The set audit found one true duplicate on the pixels (geodesic vs fullerene - fullerene's cage survives thinking and speaking, geodesic's nodes smear) and one same-idea pair (kirkwood vs orbit). Archived faces stay selectable behind a 'more faces' toggle, keep their id on every client, and receive every colour and transition fix - the owner's identity face can never vanish. Nothing is archived until the owner chooses; `faces[].archived` is the switch.",
  "options": {
   "curated": "18 visible - geodesic and kirkwood archived (recommended)",
   "tighter": "16 visible - also spectrum and workbench",
   "all": "20 visible - framing fixes only"
  }
 },
 "colour_science": {
  "delta_e_2000": {
   "ember-4 vs amber-4": {
    "normal": 12.1,
    "deuteranopia": 4.5
   },
   "ember-4 vs rose-4": {
    "normal": 24.8,
    "deuteranopia": 12.0
   },
   "amber-4 vs rose-4": {
    "normal": 36.2,
    "deuteranopia": 15.6
   },
   "verdant-4 vs rose-4": {
    "normal": 67.7,
    "deuteranopia": 8.2
   },
   "verdant-4 vs ember-4": {
    "normal": 50.1,
    "deuteranopia": 16.7
   }
  },
  "note": "CIE Delta-E 2000 is the standard score for how different two colours look: under 2 indistinguishable, ~10 obviously different at arm's length, 20+ across a room. Deuteranopia simulated with the Machado 2009 matrix. Conclusion: moving listening to green trades the listening/approval collision (4.5) for a listening/error one (8.2), so hue alone cannot separate the warm states for everyone; motion must. Option kept open for the owner: re-bind approval to verdant-5 (the randomiser already sanctions green for approval), which lifts listening/approval to 41 (18.7 deuteranope)."
 },
 "audit_history": [
  {
   "date": "2026-09-14",
   "kind": "visual (rendered images, six specialists + three cross-talks)",
   "changes": [
    "colour crossfade 300ms",
    "speech envelope + synthetic fallback",
    "approval waiting clock, tint delay",
    "thinking default cool sweep",
    "speaking default reactive ice",
    "error default rose pulse",
    "gradient: bound colour wins",
    "unified background",
    "depth_gain 1.6",
    "per-state fps",
    "glow sprite bloom (canvas)",
    "tap ack + drag inertia",
    "fit framing",
    "kirkwood sun palette",
    "coreplate never white",
    "spectrum bar cap",
    "iris catchlight",
    "flash governor per surface (bug found by the re-render)",
    "pattern-default duplication removed from _resolveRaw + flash limits clamped at the point of use (found by the Android port checking shell.html against the spec)"
   ]
  }
 ],
 "patterns_note": "Pattern defaults live HERE and only here. A renderer must read them from this file and must not carry its own copy - shell.html did, and they drifted. hue_sweep is the proof the shape was wrong rather than merely stale: rainbow and sweep share the kind with different span/sat/offset, so no single inline literal can be right for both."
}
````

````kotlin
// Versions verified against the registries on 13 Sep 2026, not inferred:
//   AGP 9.4.0            gradle-9.4.0.pom -> 200 on Google Maven
//   Kotlin               supplied BY AGP 9. The org.jetbrains.kotlin.android
//                        plugin is not applied: since AGP 9.0 it is built in,
//                        and applying it alongside is a hard error.
//   Compose BOM 2026.08.00  exists; pins androidx.compose.foundation 1.12.0
plugins {
    id("com.android.application") version "9.4.0" apply false
    id("org.jetbrains.kotlin.plugin.compose") version "2.4.20" apply false
    id("org.jetbrains.kotlin.plugin.serialization") version "2.4.20" apply false
}
````

## `jarvis-client/gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.properties`

````properties
distributionBase=GRADLE_USER_HOME
distributionPath=wrapper/dists
distributionUrl=https\://services.gradle.org/distributions/gradle-9.6.0-bin.zip
# Verified against services.gradle.org, which this dev container cannot reach;
# read from a CI runner by .github/workflows/verify-toolchain.yml.
# validateDistributionUrl only checks the URL is well formed and reachable.
# Without this line every build downloads ~130 MB and executes it unverified.
distributionSha256Sum=bbaeb2fef8710818cf0e261201dab964c572f92b942812df0c3620d62a529a01
networkTimeout=10000
validateDistributionUrl=true
zipStoreBase=GRADLE_USER_HOME
zipStorePath=wrapper/dists
````

## `jarvis-client/gradlew`

````bash
#!/bin/sh

#
# Copyright © 2015-2021 the original authors.
#
# Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
# you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
# You may obtain a copy of the License at

#
# Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
# distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
# WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
# See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
# limitations under the License.
#
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
#

##############################################################################
#
#   Gradle start up script for POSIX generated by Gradle.
#
#   Important for running:
#
#   (1) You need a POSIX-compliant shell to run this script. If your /bin/sh is
#       noncompliant, but you have some other compliant shell such as ksh or
#       bash, then to run this script, type that shell name before the whole
#       command line, like:
#
#           ksh Gradle
#
#       Busybox and similar reduced shells will NOT work, because this script
#       requires all of these POSIX shell features:
#         * functions;
#         * expansions «$var», «${var}», «${var:-default}», «${var+SET}»,
#           «${var#prefix}», «${var%suffix}», and «$( cmd )»;
#         * compound commands having a testable exit status, especially «case»;
#         * various built-in commands including «command», «set», and «ulimit».
#
#   Important for patching:
#
#   (2) This script targets any POSIX shell, so it avoids extensions provided
#       by Bash, Ksh, etc; in particular arrays are avoided.
#
#       The "traditional" practice of packing multiple parameters into a
#       space-separated string is a well documented source of bugs and security
#       problems, so this is (mostly) avoided, by progressively accumulating
#       options in "$@", and eventually passing that to Java.
#
#       Where the inherited environment variables (DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS, JAVA_OPTS,
#       and GRADLE_OPTS) rely on word-splitting, this is performed explicitly;
#       see the in-line comments for details.
#
#       There are tweaks for specific operating systems such as AIX, CygWin,
#       Darwin, MinGW, and NonStop.
#
#   (3) This script is generated from the Groovy template
#       https://github.com/gradle/gradle/blob/HEAD/platforms/jvm/plugins-application/src/main/resources/org/gradle/api/internal/plugins/unixStartScript.txt
#       within the Gradle project.
#
#       You can find Gradle at https://github.com/gradle/gradle/.
#
##############################################################################

# Attempt to set APP_HOME

# Resolve links: $0 may be a link
app_path=$0

# Need this for daisy-chained symlinks.
while
    APP_HOME=${app_path%"${app_path##*/}"}  # leaves a trailing /; empty if no leading path
    [ -h "$app_path" ]
do
    ls=$( ls -ld "$app_path" )
    link=${ls#*' -> '}
    case $link in             #(
      /*)   app_path=$link ;; #(
      *)    app_path=$APP_HOME$link ;;
    esac
done

# This is normally unused
# shellcheck disable=SC2034
APP_BASE_NAME=${0##*/}
# Discard cd standard output in case $CDPATH is set (https://github.com/gradle/gradle/issues/25036)
APP_HOME=$( cd -P "${APP_HOME:-./}" > /dev/null && printf '%s\n' "$PWD" ) || exit

# Use the maximum available, or set MAX_FD != -1 to use that value.
MAX_FD=maximum

warn () {
    echo "$*"
} >&2

die () {
    echo
    echo "$*"
    echo
    exit 1
} >&2

# OS specific support (must be 'true' or 'false').
cygwin=false
msys=false
darwin=false
nonstop=false
case "$( uname )" in                #(
  CYGWIN* )         cygwin=true  ;; #(
  Darwin* )         darwin=true  ;; #(
  MSYS* | MINGW* )  msys=true    ;; #(
  NONSTOP* )        nonstop=true ;;
esac

CLASSPATH="\\\"\\\""


# Determine the Java command to use to start the JVM.
if [ -n "$JAVA_HOME" ] ; then
    if [ -x "$JAVA_HOME/jre/sh/java" ] ; then
        # IBM's JDK on AIX uses strange locations for the executables
        JAVACMD=$JAVA_HOME/jre/sh/java
    else
        JAVACMD=$JAVA_HOME/bin/java
    fi
    if [ ! -x "$JAVACMD" ] ; then
        die "ERROR: JAVA_HOME is set to an invalid directory: $JAVA_HOME

Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
location of your Java installation."
    fi
else
    JAVACMD=java
    if ! command -v java >/dev/null 2>&1
    then
        die "ERROR: JAVA_HOME is not set and no 'java' command could be found in your PATH.

Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
location of your Java installation."
    fi
fi

# Increase the maximum file descriptors if we can.
if ! "$cygwin" && ! "$darwin" && ! "$nonstop" ; then
    case $MAX_FD in #(
      max*)
        # In POSIX sh, ulimit -H is undefined. That's why the result is checked to see if it worked.
        # shellcheck disable=SC2039,SC3045
        MAX_FD=$( ulimit -H -n ) ||
            warn "Could not query maximum file descriptor limit"
    esac
    case $MAX_FD in  #(
      '' | soft) :;; #(
      *)
        # In POSIX sh, ulimit -n is undefined. That's why the result is checked to see if it worked.
        # shellcheck disable=SC2039,SC3045
        ulimit -n "$MAX_FD" ||
            warn "Could not set maximum file descriptor limit to $MAX_FD"
    esac
fi

# Collect all arguments for the java command, stacking in reverse order:
#   * args from the command line
#   * the main class name
#   * -classpath
#   * -D...appname settings
#   * --module-path (only if needed)
#   * DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS, JAVA_OPTS, and GRADLE_OPTS environment variables.

# For Cygwin or MSYS, switch paths to Windows format before running java
if "$cygwin" || "$msys" ; then
    APP_HOME=$( cygpath --path --mixed "$APP_HOME" )
    CLASSPATH=$( cygpath --path --mixed "$CLASSPATH" )

    JAVACMD=$( cygpath --unix "$JAVACMD" )

    # Now convert the arguments - kludge to limit ourselves to /bin/sh
    for arg do
        if
            case $arg in                                #(
              -*)   false ;;                            # don't mess with options #(
              /?*)  t=${arg#/} t=/${t%%/*}              # looks like a POSIX filepath
                    [ -e "$t" ] ;;                      #(
              *)    false ;;
            esac
        then
            arg=$( cygpath --path --ignore --mixed "$arg" )
        fi
        # Roll the args list around exactly as many times as the number of
        # args, so each arg winds up back in the position where it started, but
        # possibly modified.
        #
        # NB: a `for` loop captures its iteration list before it begins, so
        # changing the positional parameters here affects neither the number of
        # iterations, nor the values presented in `arg`.
        shift                   # remove old arg
        set -- "$@" "$arg"      # push replacement arg
    done
fi


# Add default JVM options here. You can also use JAVA_OPTS and GRADLE_OPTS to pass JVM options to this script.
DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS='"-Xmx64m" "-Xms64m"'

# Collect all arguments for the java command:
#   * DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS, JAVA_OPTS, and optsEnvironmentVar are not allowed to contain shell fragments,
#     and any embedded shellness will be escaped.
#   * For example: A user cannot expect ${Hostname} to be expanded, as it is an environment variable and will be
#     treated as '${Hostname}' itself on the command line.

set -- \
        "-Dorg.gradle.appname=$APP_BASE_NAME" \
        -classpath "$CLASSPATH" \
        -jar "$APP_HOME/gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.jar" \
        "$@"

# Stop when "xargs" is not available.
if ! command -v xargs >/dev/null 2>&1
then
    die "xargs is not available"
fi

# Use "xargs" to parse quoted args.
#
# With -n1 it outputs one arg per line, with the quotes and backslashes removed.
#
# In Bash we could simply go:
#
#   readarray ARGS < <( xargs -n1 <<<"$var" ) &&
#   set -- "${ARGS[@]}" "$@"
#
# but POSIX shell has neither arrays nor command substitution, so instead we
# post-process each arg (as a line of input to sed) to backslash-escape any
# character that might be a shell metacharacter, then use eval to reverse
# that process (while maintaining the separation between arguments), and wrap
# the whole thing up as a single "set" statement.
#
# This will of course break if any of these variables contains a newline or
# an unmatched quote.
#

eval "set -- $(
        printf '%s\n' "$DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS $JAVA_OPTS $GRADLE_OPTS" |
        xargs -n1 |
        sed ' s~[^-[:alnum:]+,./:=@_]~\\&~g; ' |
        tr '\n' ' '
    )" '"$@"'

exec "$JAVACMD" "$@"
````

## `jarvis-client/gradlew.bat`

````
@rem
@rem Copyright 2015 the original author or authors.
@rem
@rem Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
@rem you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
@rem You may obtain a copy of the License at
@rem
@rem      https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
@rem
@rem Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
@rem distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
@rem WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
@rem See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
@rem limitations under the License.
@rem
@rem SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
@rem

@if "%DEBUG%"=="" @echo off
@rem ##########################################################################
@rem
@rem  Gradle startup script for Windows
@rem
@rem ##########################################################################

@rem Set local scope for the variables with windows NT shell
if "%OS%"=="Windows_NT" setlocal

set DIRNAME=%~dp0
if "%DIRNAME%"=="" set DIRNAME=.
@rem This is normally unused
set APP_BASE_NAME=%~n0
set APP_HOME=%DIRNAME%

@rem Resolve any "." and ".." in APP_HOME to make it shorter.
for %%i in ("%APP_HOME%") do set APP_HOME=%%~fi

@rem Add default JVM options here. You can also use JAVA_OPTS and GRADLE_OPTS to pass JVM options to this script.
set DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS="-Xmx64m" "-Xms64m"

@rem Find java.exe
if defined JAVA_HOME goto findJavaFromJavaHome

set JAVA_EXE=java.exe
%JAVA_EXE% -version >NUL 2>&1
if %ERRORLEVEL% equ 0 goto execute

echo. 1>&2
echo ERROR: JAVA_HOME is not set and no 'java' command could be found in your PATH. 1>&2
echo. 1>&2
echo Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the 1>&2
echo location of your Java installation. 1>&2

goto fail

:findJavaFromJavaHome
set JAVA_HOME=%JAVA_HOME:"=%
set JAVA_EXE=%JAVA_HOME%/bin/java.exe

if exist "%JAVA_EXE%" goto execute

echo. 1>&2
echo ERROR: JAVA_HOME is set to an invalid directory: %JAVA_HOME% 1>&2
echo. 1>&2
echo Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the 1>&2
echo location of your Java installation. 1>&2

goto fail

:execute
@rem Setup the command line

set CLASSPATH=


@rem Execute Gradle
"%JAVA_EXE%" %DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS% %JAVA_OPTS% %GRADLE_OPTS% "-Dorg.gradle.appname=%APP_BASE_NAME%" -classpath "%CLASSPATH%" -jar "%APP_HOME%\gradle\wrapper\gradle-wrapper.jar" %*

:end
@rem End local scope for the variables with windows NT shell
if %ERRORLEVEL% equ 0 goto mainEnd

:fail
rem Set variable GRADLE_EXIT_CONSOLE if you need the _script_ return code instead of
rem the _cmd.exe /c_ return code!
set EXIT_CODE=%ERRORLEVEL%
if %EXIT_CODE% equ 0 set EXIT_CODE=1
if not ""=="%GRADLE_EXIT_CONSOLE%" exit %EXIT_CODE%
exit /b %EXIT_CODE%

:mainEnd
if "%OS%"=="Windows_NT" endlocal

:omega
````

## `keystore/README.md`

````markdown
# Debug signing key

`debug.keystore` is the key both apps are signed with for sideloading. It is
committed deliberately, and it is not a secret: it uses the standard Android
debug credentials (`android` / `android`, alias `androiddebugkey`), which are
published in Google's own documentation.

## Why it is here

Without it, AGP generates `~/.android/debug.keystore` on first use, and a
GitHub runner is a fresh machine every time — so every CI run signed the APK
with a **different** key. `adb install -r` of a new build over an old one then
fails with `INSTALL_FAILED_UPDATE_INCOMPATIBLE`, and the only way through is
`adb uninstall`, which wipes app data.

App data here is the pairing secret, the auth token, the device id and any
queued notes or approval decisions. So every upgrade cost a re-pair, and any
decision still sitting in the offline queue was lost. A fixed key makes
upgrades install in place and keeps all of it.

## What it is not

It is not a release key and there is no release build — the app is sideloaded
over adb and never listed, so there is nothing for a release key to sign. Do
not reuse this key if that ever changes: anyone who can read this repository
can sign an APK with it.

Fingerprint (SHA-256):

    A5:8C:14:56:F9:4C:C3:E2:84:06:CC:D6:FD:0A:67:5F:32:F8:CE:5C:70:28:E0:7B:0B:F9:97:05:8F:48:29:00

Valid until 2056-09-06.
````
